{"type":"tool_execution_end","toolCallId":"chatcmpl-tool-215a3a1fe68c4bbdbbbe3cce465c4cda","toolName":"read","result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"# Route parked escalations through the escalate-resume gate\n\n> Parent roadmap: `roadmap-supervisor-orchestration`\n> Roadmap item: `ri-06`\n> Change ID: `route-parked-escalations-through-the-escalate-resume-gate`\n\n## Why\n\nA phase retry-budget exhaustion is durably collected as `parked.kind: policy_pause`, but it is not routed until a later session discovers it. The prior ri-06 plan correctly moved routing after complete batch application, but plan review found two blockers: its gate persistence could overwrite a concurrent checkpoint transition after approval-service waiting, and a resumed member of a multi-member batch could never apply its fresh result once peers had completed.\n\nThis recovery retains the immediate escalation outcome while defining one authoritative checkpoint transaction boundary and a safe current-generation result set.\n\n## What Changes\n\nAfter every complete delegated-batch application, a retryable routing operation processes parked `policy_pause` attempts through the existing `gate_router.resolve_parked` seam. It routes only after a runtime-enforced complete-`effects_applied` batch check; partial application recovery never evaluates or notifies a gate.\n\nThe roadmap runtime gains a shared, process-safe checkpoint transaction used by every checkpoint read-modify-write path. It serializes only short fresh reads, authoritative decision/attempt updates, and derived mirror reconciliation—never approval-service I/O. Gate routing holds a generation-scoped subject lock through one external evaluation, then rechecks the current subject inside the shared transaction. A valid proceed records its decision and advances the exact parked attempt to its next lease generation atomically; a stale candidate has no durable or mirror effect. Gate decisions become part of that one checkpoint serialization rather than a post-save sidecar rewrite.\n\nDelegated apply changes its result-membership contract: results must exactly cover all attempts whose *current* application journal is not `effects_applied`. Already-applied peers are deliberately omitted and never revalidated or replayed. That preserves initial-batch exactness and at-most-once callbacks while admitting a fresh result for a resumed member after peer cleanup.\n\n## Selected Approach\n\nUse a shared checkpoint transaction plus a per-`escalate_resume` generation subject lock, and define the fresh-application batch subset by durable journal state.\n\nThis approach prevents stale whole-checkpoint writes without holding the workspace state lock during network waits, makes decision-plus-resume an atomic authority update, and fixes multi-member resume without inventing a new batch identity or weakening evidence validation.\n\n### Approaches Considered\n\n1. **Fresh append after approval wait** — reload the checkpoint only before appending `gate_decisions`.\n   - Pros: Small code change.\n   - Cons: Still leaves a decision-to-resume race and the separate sidecar write; cannot make mirror projection safe.\n   - Effort: M.\n\n2. **Hold the existing workspace lock while evaluating approval** — serialize all activity through the current execution lock.\n   - Pros: Prevents stale writes.\n   - Cons: Blocks unrelated transition work for a notify timeout and violates the intended availability property.\n   - Effort: S.\n\n3. **Shared short transaction with generation subject lock** — selected.\n   - Pros: Preserves unrelated progress during external I/O; commits fresh decision/resume state atomically; gives a deterministic recovery path.\n   - Cons: Requires migrating every checkpoint writer to one lock/serialization seam and explicit stale-candidate handling.\n   - Effort: L.\n\n## Impact\n\n- `skills/roadmap-runtime/scripts/checkpoint.py` and models: shared checkpoint transaction and one atomic gate-decision serialization.\n- `skills/supervise/scripts/gate_router.py`, `execution.py`, `cycle_state.py`: generation-scoped routing, atomic authorized resume, legacy lookup, derived mirror reconciliation, and shared sanitized context.\n- `skills/autopilot-roadmap/scripts/orchestrator.py`: current-journal delegated-result membership.\n- `openspec/schemas/gate-decision.schema.json`: optional positive `lease_generation` correlation.\n- `skills/tests/roadmap-runtime`, `skills/tests/supervise`, and `skills/tests/autopilot-roadmap`: deterministic concurrency and resumed-batch regressions.\n- `openspec/specs/supervise/spec.md` and `openspec/specs/roadmap-orchestration/spec.md`: behavior deltas.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Changing Autopilot's retry budget, ESCALATE state machine, or notification channel.\n- Automatically routing ordinary child `pending_gate` results or quarantined attempts.\n- Adding a new batch identifier, weakening exact evidence validation, or replaying an already-applied peer callback.\n- Changing the separate, already-completed always-on roadmap item with the same local number.\n\n## Acceptance Outcomes\n\n- A complete batch's `policy_pause` immediately produces one generation-scoped `escalate_resume` decision; a partial batch never does.\n- A blocked approval wait cannot erase unrelated checkpoint transitions, and concurrent resolutions cannot duplicate a decision, notification, or resume.\n- A valid proceed decision and the parked-to-prepared generation transition are authoritative together; mirror loss is recoverable from the checkpoint ledger.\n- A resumed member in a multi-member original batch can apply its current-generation result after completed peers are cleaned up, while missing current results and historical peer results fail before any callback.\n- Legacy generationless escalation records are reused for their current parked generation without duplicate filing; later generations reject stale approval references.\n"}]},"isError":false}

{"type":"tool_execution_end","toolCallId":"chatcmpl-tool-e233a7ca6b914a87aaa693841d7d56ff","toolName":"read","result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"# Design — Route parked escalations through the escalate-resume gate\n\n## Context\n\nThe original ri-06 design was stopped before implementation because a stale pre-wait checkpoint snapshot could overwrite a concurrent transition, and its exact-whole-batch result rule stranded resumed members. The checkpoint is the execution authority; the supervisor mirror and handoff are derived views. This design closes both authority boundaries before immediate routing is introduced.\n\n## Decisions\n\n### D1 — Route only after the runtime proves complete batch application\n\n`ExecutionAdapter.route_parked_escalations(workspace, batch_id, repo_root, evaluator=None)` SHALL load the named batch and verify every member is terminal with `application_journal.state == effects_applied` before it evaluates, files, projects, or resumes anything. It scans only parked `policy_pause` attempts in stable dispatch-ID order and reports already-prepared policy-pause continuations as `already_routed`.\n\nA partial apply error may expose bounded candidate IDs for diagnostics, but SHALL not call routing. Recovery replays apply through the journal first; after the complete-apply boundary a routing failure retries routing only. Ordinary `pending_gate`, failed, and quarantined attempts remain excluded.\n\n### D2 — One shared checkpoint transaction is the authority boundary\n\nRoadmap runtime SHALL expose one process-safe per-workspace transaction mechanism. It uses the same lock namespace for execution, router, and direct orchestrator checkpoint writers, loads the current checkpoint *inside* the lock, invokes a bounded mutation callback, and writes one atomic checkpoint payload. `gate_decisions` becomes a serialized checkpoint field in that same payload; there is no post-save sidecar read/overwrite step.\n\nEvery current checkpoint read-modify-write path migrates to this mechanism or a non-reentrant helper invoked under it. The old execution-private lock is moved behind this shared interface so router and execution cannot serialize different snapshots. The transaction lock never covers approval service calls, notification polling, host callback execution, or other network I/O.\n\n### D3 — Subject lock plus fresh-CAS commits the decision and proceed transition\n\n`escalate_resume` has a process-safe per-subject lock keyed by roadmap, dispatch ID, and parked lease generation. Automatic routing, manual resolution, and answering use the same subject lock. The holder may evaluate/check a prior approval outside the workspace transaction. Before recording a new outcome it enters the shared checkpoint transaction, reloads current state, re-applies the prior-record rule, and verifies the exact attempt is still parked with the expected `policy_pause` and generation.\n\nFor a blocked decision, the fresh transaction appends one decision record. For proceed, the same transaction appends the record and converts that exact attempt from parked G to prepared G+1, clearing its generation-G terminal fields and journal. It returns the durable continuation request. A candidate that no longer matches is stale: it does not append, project, or resume. A concurrent resolver reuses the winning current record rather than filing another approval.\n\nThe router validates a pending-gate projection before authority commit so refusal creates no record. It projects derived mirror state only after the authoritative transaction; the short transaction serializes mirror read/merge/write for different subjects. Projection failure never rolls back authority and is reconciled idempotently from the ledger on rehydrate/gate-log.\n\n### D4 — Escalate identity is generation-aware and legacy-safe\n\nNew `escalate_resume` records carry optional positive integer `lease_generation`. Subject lookup, late answers, approval references, mirror retirement, and optional `gate-answer --lease-generation` all use it. A dispatch-only answer selects the newest blocked generation. `require_approval_ref` rejects a proceed whose recorded generation differs from the currently parked generation.\n\nA legacy generationless `escalate_resume` record matches the current parked generation once, is reused rather than refiled, and is retired with older same-dispatch mirror entries once a newer generation is recorded. Other gate identities remain unchanged.\n\n### D5 — Policy-pause context is always sanitized at the shared seam\n\nEvery `policy_pause` call to `resolve_parked`, automatic or manual, builds exactly:\n`dispatch_id, change_id, item_id, lease_generation, verb: resume, reason: supervised phase retry budget exhausted`.\nValues come from the current validated attempt. Child-provided reason, transcript, raw approval response, and additional keys never enter outbound context, route output, checkpoint record, or pending mirror entry. Blocked output contains only normalized pending-gate data.\n\n### D6 — Current journal state defines required apply membership\n\nFor a named batch, `apply_delegated_batch` derives its required result IDs from attempts whose current journal state is not `effects_applied`. Submitted IDs must exactly equal that nonempty set before callbacks or state mutation. Omitted attempts are valid only when already `effects_applied`; submitted results retain all current identity, generation, isolation, evidence, digest, and at-most-once journal checks.\n\nThus an untouched initial batch still requires every member. After A parks, B completes, and A is authorized to G+1, only A's current result is admitted. A historical B result is rejected rather than revalidated, and a missing resumed member fails before any callback. A terminal-persisted-but-not-effects-applied attempt remains required for crash recovery.\n\n## Test Strategy\n\n1. Block routing after its evaluation snapshot, concurrently commit unrelated child transition and apply mutations, then prove both survive with exactly one decision record.\n2. Change the target attempt while routing waits and prove the stale candidate cannot resume, append, or project.\n3. Race automatic/manual resolution and prove one evaluation, notification, decision, and generation bump; prove two different blocked subjects retain both mirror entries.\n4. Inject a checkpoint-save boundary and prove visible state has either old or new checkpoint-plus-gate-decision payload, never a separate sidecar state.\n5. Verify complete-batch guard, post-apply route retry, exact sanitized context, exclusions, legacy reuse, generation answer selection, and stale-ref rejection.\n6. Prove two-member park → route → G+1 resume → fresh A-only apply after B cleanup; cover multiple resumed members, missing subset, historical peer, stale generation, and terminal-persisted recovery.\n"}]},"isError":false}
{"type":"tool_execution_end","toolCallId":"chatcmpl-tool-f16edb0bfb3943fda94c8546eefeecf8","toolName":"read","result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"# Contracts\n\nThis change has no closed HTTP, database, or event contract. It changes two in-process durable contracts.\n\n## Checkpoint transaction\n\nThe checkpoint ledger is the execution authority. A shared per-workspace transaction loads current checkpoint state under the common process-safe lock and writes one atomic payload containing all state and `gate_decisions`. A caller may evaluate an approval outside that transaction, but must recheck its exact dispatch, parked kind, and lease generation inside it before appending a decision or creating a continuation. Mirror and handoff are derived, repairable projections.\n\n## Escalate resume\n\nNew `escalate_resume` decision records carry optional positive integer `lease_generation`; generationless legacy records remain readable and reusable for the current parked generation. A `gate-decision:<decision_id>` proceed reference authorizes the same dispatch and parked generation only.\n\nAutomatic and manual policy-pause context contains exactly:\n`dispatch_id`, `change_id`, `item_id`, `lease_generation`, `verb: \"resume\"`, and `reason: \"supervised phase retry budget exhausted\"`.\n\n`route_parked_escalations` returns at most one bounded entry per batch attempt:\n- all entries: `dispatch_id`, `outcome`, `decided_lease_generation`;\n- `proceed` or `already_routed`: additionally `resumed_lease_generation`;\n- `blocked`: additionally one supervisor-normalized `pending_gate`.\n\nNo route input, durable record, or result may contain child transcript, raw approval data, child-provided reason, or additional context keys.\n\n## Delegated apply cohort\n\nFor a named batch, apply requires results exactly for attempts whose current application-journal state is not `effects_applied`. All submitted results still require the current dispatch identity, lease generation, isolation, evidence, and journal digest. Already-applied peers are omitted and rejected if submitted.\n"}]},"isError":false}
{"type":"message_start","message":{"role":"toolResult","toolCallId":"chatcmpl-tool-215a3a1fe68c4bbdbbbe3cce465c4cda","toolName":"read","content":[{"type":"text","text":"# Route parked escalations through the escalate-resume gate\n\n> Parent roadmap: `roadmap-supervisor-orchestration`\n> Roadmap item: `ri-06`\n> Change ID: `route-parked-escalations-through-the-escalate-resume-gate`\n\n## Why\n\nA phase retry-budget exhaustion is durably collected as `parked.kind: policy_pause`, but it is not routed until a later session discovers it. The prior ri-06 plan correctly moved routing after complete batch application, but plan review found two blockers: its gate persistence could overwrite a concurrent checkpoint transition after approval-service waiting, and a resumed member of a multi-member batch could never apply its fresh result once peers had completed.\n\nThis recovery retains the immediate escalation outcome while defining one authoritative checkpoint transaction boundary and a safe current-generation result set.\n\n## What Changes\n\nAfter every complete delegated-batch application, a retryable routing operation processes parked `policy_pause` attempts through the existing `gate_router.resolve_parked` seam. It routes only after a runtime-enforced complete-`effects_applied` batch check; partial application recovery never evaluates or notifies a gate.\n\nThe roadmap runtime gains a shared, process-safe checkpoint transaction used by every checkpoint read-modify-write path. It serializes only short fresh reads, authoritative decision/attempt updates, and derived mirror reconciliation—never approval-service I/O. Gate routing holds a generation-scoped subject lock through one external evaluation, then rechecks the current subject inside the shared transaction. A valid proceed records its decision and advances the exact parked attempt to its next lease generation atomically; a stale candidate has no durable or mirror effect. Gate decisions become part of that one checkpoint serialization rather than a post-save sidecar rewrite.\n\nDelegated apply changes its result-membership contract: results must exactly cover all attempts whose *current* application journal is not `effects_applied`. Already-applied peers are deliberately omitted and never revalidated or replayed. That preserves initial-batch exactness and at-most-once callbacks while admitting a fresh result for a resumed member after peer cleanup.\n\n## Selected Approach\n\nUse a shared checkpoint transaction plus a per-`escalate_resume` generation subject lock, and define the fresh-application batch subset by durable journal state.\n\nThis approach prevents stale whole-checkpoint writes without holding the workspace state lock during network waits, makes decision-plus-resume an atomic authority update, and fixes multi-member resume without inventing a new batch identity or weakening evidence validation.\n\n### Approaches Considered\n\n1. **Fresh append after approval wait** — reload the checkpoint only before appending `gate_decisions`.\n   - Pros: Small code change.\n   - Cons: Still leaves a decision-to-resume race and the separate sidecar write; cannot make mirror projection safe.\n   - Effort: M.\n\n2. **Hold the existing workspace lock while evaluating approval** — serialize all activity through the current execution lock.\n   - Pros: Prevents stale writes.\n   - Cons: Blocks unrelated transition work for a notify timeout and violates the intended availability property.\n   - Effort: S.\n\n3. **Shared short transaction with generation subject lock** — selected.\n   - Pros: Preserves unrelated progress during external I/O; commits fresh decision/resume state atomically; gives a deterministic recovery path.\n   - Cons: Requires migrating every checkpoint writer to one lock/serialization seam and explicit stale-candidate handling.\n   - Effort: L.\n\n## Impact\n\n- `skills/roadmap-runtime/scripts/checkpoint.py` and models: shared checkpoint transaction and one atomic gate-decision serialization.\n- `skills/supervise/scripts/gate_router.py`, `execution.py`, `cycle_state.py`: generation-scoped routing, atomic authorized resume, legacy lookup, derived mirror reconciliation, and shared sanitized context.\n- `skills/autopilot-roadmap/scripts/orchestrator.py`: current-journal delegated-result membership.\n- `openspec/schemas/gate-decision.schema.json`: optional positive `lease_generation` correlation.\n- `skills/tests/roadmap-runtime`, `skills/tests/supervise`, and `skills/tests/autopilot-roadmap`: deterministic concurrency and resumed-batch regressions.\n- `openspec/specs/supervise/spec.md` and `openspec/specs/roadmap-orchestration/spec.md`: behavior deltas.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Changing Autopilot's retry budget, ESCALATE state machine, or notification channel.\n- Automatically routing ordinary child `pending_gate` results or quarantined attempts.\n- Adding a new batch identifier, weakening exact evidence validation, or replaying an already-applied peer callback.\n- Changing the separate, already-completed always-on roadmap item with the same local number.\n\n## Acceptance Outcomes\n\n- A complete batch's `policy_pause` immediately produces one generation-scoped `escalate_resume` decision; a partial batch never does.\n- A blocked approval wait cannot erase unrelated checkpoint transitions, and concurrent resolutions cannot duplicate a decision, notification, or resume.\n- A valid proceed decision and the parked-to-prepared generation transition are authoritative together; mirror loss is recoverable from the checkpoint ledger.\n- A resumed member in a multi-member original batch can apply its current-generation result after completed peers are cleaned up, while missing current results and historical peer results fail before any callback.\n- Legacy generationless escalation records are reused for their current parked generation without duplicate filing; later generations reject stale approval references.\n"}],"isError":false,"timestamp":1789401349785}}

Automatic routing, manual resolution, and answering use the same subject lock. The holder may evaluate/check a prior approval outside the workspace transaction. It returns the durable continuation request. A candidate that no longer matches is stale: it does not append, project, or resume. A concurrent resolver reuses the winning current record rather than filing another approval.\n\nThe router validates a pending-gate projection before authority commit so refusal creates no record. It projects derived mirror state only after the authoritative transaction; the short transaction serializes mirror read/merge/write for different subjects. Projection failure never rolls back authority and is reconciled idempotently from the ledger on rehydrate/gate-log.\n\n### D4 — Escalate identity is generation-aware and legacy-safe\n\nNew `escalate_resume` records carry optional positive integer `lease_generation`. Blocked output contains only normalized pending-gate data.\n\n### D6 — Current journal state defines required apply membership\n\nFor a named batch, `apply_delegated_batch` derives its required result IDs from attempts whose current journal state is not `effects_applied`. {"type":"message_end","message":{"role":"toolResult","toolCallId":"chatcmpl-tool-e233a7ca6b914a87aaa693841d7d56ff","toolName":"read","content":[{"type":"text","text":"# Design — Route parked escalations through the escalate-resume gate\n\n## Context\n\nThe original ri-06 design was stopped before implementation because a stale pre-wait checkpoint snapshot could overwrite a concurrent transition, and its exact-whole-batch result rule stranded resumed members. The checkpoint is the execution authority; the supervisor mirror and handoff are derived views. This design closes both authority boundaries before immediate routing is introduced.\n\n## Decisions\n\n### D1 — Route only after the runtime proves complete batch application\n\n`ExecutionAdapter.route_parked_escalations(workspace, batch_id, repo_root, evaluator=None)` SHALL load the named batch and verify every member is terminal with `application_journal.state == effects_applied` before it evaluates, files, projects, or resumes anything. It scans only parked `policy_pause` attempts in stable dispatch-ID order and reports already-prepared policy-pause continuations as `already_routed`.\n\nA partial apply error may expose bounded candidate IDs for diagnostics, but SHALL not call routing. Recovery replays apply through the journal first; after the complete-apply boundary a routing failure retries routing only. Ordinary `pending_gate`, failed, and quarantined attempts remain excluded.\n\n### D2 — One shared checkpoint transaction is the authority boundary\n\nRoadmap runtime SHALL expose one process-safe per-workspace transaction mechanism. It uses the same lock namespace for execution, router, and direct orchestrator checkpoint writers, loads the current checkpoint *inside* the lock, invokes a bounded mutation callback, and writes one atomic checkpoint payload. `gate_decisions` becomes a serialized checkpoint field in that same payload; there is no post-save sidecar read/overwrite step.\n\nEvery current checkpoint read-modify-write path migrates to this mechanism or a non-reentrant helper invoked under it. The old execution-private lock is moved behind this shared interface so router and execution cannot serialize different snapshots. The transaction lock never covers approval service calls, notification polling, host callback execution, or other network I/O.\n\n### D3 — Subject lock plus fresh-CAS commits the decision and proceed transition\n\n`escalate_resume` has a process-safe per-subject lock keyed by roadmap, dispatch ID, and parked lease generation. Automatic routing, manual resolution, and answering use the same subject lock. The holder may evaluate/check a prior approval outside the workspace transaction. Before recording a new outcome it enters the shared checkpoint transaction, reloads current state, re-applies the prior-record rule, and verifies the exact attempt is still parked with the expected `policy_pause` and generation.\n\nFor a blocked decision, the fresh transaction appends one decision record. For proceed, the same transaction appends the record and converts that exact attempt from parked G to prepared G+1, clearing its generation-G terminal fields and journal. It returns the durable continuation request. A candidate that no longer matches is stale: it does not append, project, or resume. A concurrent resolver reuses the winning current record rather than filing another approval.\n\nThe router validates a pending-gate projection before authority commit so refusal creates no record. It projects derived mirror state only after the authoritative transaction; the short transaction serializes mirror read/merge/write for different subjects. Projection failure never rolls back authority and is reconciled idempotently from the ledger on rehydrate/gate-log.\n\n### D4 — Escalate identity is generation-aware and legacy-safe\n\nNew `escalate_resume` records carry optional positive integer `lease_generation`. Subject lookup, late answers, approval references, mirror retirement, and optional `gate-answer --lease-generation` all use it. A dispatch-only answer selects the newest blocked generation. `require_approval_ref` rejects a proceed whose recorded generation differs from the currently parked generation.\n\nA legacy generationless `escalate_resume` record matches the current parked generation once, is reused rather than refiled, and is retired with older same-dispatch mirror entries once a newer generation is recorded. Other gate identities remain unchanged.\n\n### D5 — Policy-pause context is always sanitized at the shared seam\n\nEvery `policy_pause` call to `resolve_parked`, automatic or manual, builds exactly:\n`dispatch_id, change_id, item_id, lease_generation, verb: resume, reason: supervised phase retry budget exhausted`.\nValues come from the current validated attempt. Child-provided reason, transcript, raw approval response, and additional keys never enter outbound context, route output, checkpoint record, or pending mirror entry. Blocked output contains only normalized pending-gate data.\n\n### D6 — Current journal state defines required apply membership\n\nFor a named batch, `apply_delegated_batch` derives its required result IDs from attempts whose current journal state is not `effects_applied`. Submitted IDs must exactly equal that nonempty set before callbacks or state mutation. Omitted attempts are valid only when already `effects_applied`; submitted results retain all current identity, generation, isolation, evidence, digest, and at-most-once journal checks.\n\nThus an untouched initial batch still requires every member. After A parks, B completes, and A is authorized to G+1, only A's current result is admitted. A historical B result is rejected rather than revalidated, and a missing resumed member fails before any callback. A terminal-persisted-but-not-effects-applied attempt remains required for crash recovery.\n\n## Test Strategy\n\n1. Block routing after its evaluation snapshot, concurrently commit unrelated child transition and apply mutations, then prove both survive with exactly one decision record.\n2. Change the target attempt while routing waits and prove the stale candidate cannot resume, append, or project.\n3. Race automatic/manual resolution and prove one evaluation, notification, decision, and generation bump; prove two different blocked subjects retain both mirror entries.\n4. Inject a checkpoint-save boundary and prove visible state has either old or new checkpoint-plus-gate-decision payload, never a separate sidecar state.\n5. Verify complete-batch guard, post-apply route retry, exact sanitized context, exclusions, legacy reuse, generation answer selection, and stale-ref rejection.\n6. Prove two-member park → route → G+1 resume → fresh A-only apply after B cleanup; cover multiple resumed members, missing subset, historical peer, stale generation, and terminal-persisted recovery.\n"}],"isError":false,"timestamp":1789401349785}}

Route parked escalations through the escalate-resume gate\n\n> Parent roadmap: `roadmap-supervisor-orchestration`\n> Roadmap item: `ri-06`\n> Change ID: `route-parked-escalations-through-the-escalate-resume-gate`\n\n## Why\n\nA phase retry-budget exhaustion is durably collected as `parked.kind: policy_pause`, but it is not routed until a later session discovers it. The prior ri-06 plan correctly moved routing after complete batch application, but plan review found two blockers: its gate persistence could overwrite a concurrent checkpoint transition after approval-service waiting, and a resumed member of a multi-member batch could never apply its fresh result once peers had completed.\n\nThis recovery retains the immediate escalation outcome while defining one authoritative checkpoint transaction boundary and a safe current-generation result set.\n\n## What Changes\n\nAfter every complete delegated-batch application, a retryable routing operation processes parked `policy_pause` attempts through the existing `gate_router.resolve_parked` seam. It routes only after a runtime-enforced complete-`effects_applied` batch check; partial application recovery never evaluates or notifies a gate.\n\nThe roadmap runtime gains a shared, process-safe checkpoint transaction used by every checkpoint read-modify-write path. It serializes only short fresh reads, authoritative decision/attempt updates, and derived mirror reconciliation—never approval-service I/O. Gate routing holds a generation-scoped subject lock through one external evaluation, then rechecks the current subject inside the shared transaction. A valid proceed records its decision and advances the exact parked attempt to its next lease generation atomically; a stale candidate has no durable or mirror effect. Gate decisions become part of that one checkpoint serialization rather than a post-save sidecar rewrite.\n\nDelegated apply changes its result-membership contract: results must exactly cover all attempts whose *current* application journal is not `effects_applied`. Already-applied peers are deliberately omitted and never revalidated or replayed. That preserves initial-batch exactness and at-most-once callbacks while admitting a fresh result for a resumed member after peer cleanup.\n\n## Selected Approach\n\nUse a shared checkpoint transaction plus a per-`escalate_resume` generation subject lock, and define the fresh-application batch subset by durable journal state.\n\nThis approach prevents stale whole-checkpoint writes without holding the workspace state lock during network waits, makes decision-plus-resume an atomic authority update, and fixes multi-member resume without inventing a new batch identity or weakening evidence validation.\n\n### Approaches Considered\n\n1. **Fresh append after approval wait** — reload the checkpoint only before appending `gate_decisions`.\n   - Pros: Small code change.\n   - Cons: Still leaves a decision-to-resume race and the separate sidecar write; cannot make mirror projection safe.\n   - Effort: M.\n\n2. **Hold the existing workspace lock while evaluating approval** — serialize all activity through the current execution lock.\n   - Pros: Prevents stale writes.\n   - Cons: Blocks unrelated transition work for a notify timeout and violates the intended availability property.\n   - Effort: S.\n\n3. **Shared short transaction with generation subject lock** — selected.\n   - Pros: Preserves unrelated progress during external I/O; commits fresh decision/resume state atomically; gives a deterministic recovery path.\n   - Cons: Requires migrating every checkpoint writer to one lock/serialization seam and explicit stale-candidate handling.\n   - Effort: L.\n\n## Impact\n\n- `skills/roadmap-runtime/scripts/checkpoint.py` and models: shared checkpoint transaction and one atomic gate-decision serialization.\n- `skills/supervise/scripts/gate_router.py`, `execution.py`, `cycle_state.py`: generation-scoped routing, atomic authorized resume, legacy lookup, derived mirror reconciliation, and shared sanitized context.\n- `skills/autopilot-roadmap/scripts/orchestrator.py`: current-journal delegated-result membership.\n- `openspec/schemas/gate-decision.schema.json`: optional positive `lease_generation` correlation.\n- `skills/tests/roadmap-runtime`, `skills/tests/supervise`, and `skills/tests/autopilot-roadmap`: deterministic concurrency and resumed-batch regressions.\n- `openspec/specs/supervise/spec.md` and `openspec/specs/roadmap-orchestration/spec.md`: behavior deltas.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Changing Autopilot's retry budget, ESCALATE state machine, or notification channel.\n- Automatically routing ordinary child `pending_gate` results or quarantined attempts.\n- Adding a new batch identifier, weakening exact evidence validation, or replaying an already-applied peer callback.\n- Changing the separate, already-completed always-on roadmap item with the same local number.\n\n## Acceptance Outcomes\n\n- A complete batch's `policy_pause` immediately produces one generation-scoped `escalate_resume` decision; a partial batch never does.\n- A blocked approval wait cannot erase unrelated checkpoint transitions, and concurrent resolutions cannot duplicate a decision, notification, or resume.\n- A valid proceed decision and the parked-to-prepared generation transition are authoritative together; mirror loss is recoverable from the checkpoint ledger.\n- A resumed member in a multi-member original batch can apply its current-generation result after completed peers are cleaned up, while missing current results and historical peer results fail before any callback.\n- Legacy generationless escalation records are reused for their current parked generation without duplicate filing; later generations reject stale approval references.\n"}],"isError":false,"timestamp":1789401349785},{"role":"toolResult","toolCallId":"chatcmpl-tool-e233a7ca6b914a87aaa693841d7d56ff","toolName":"read","content":[{"type":"text","text":"# Design — Route parked escalations through the escalate-resume gate\n\n## Context\n\nThe original ri-06 design was stopped before implementation because a stale pre-wait checkpoint snapshot could overwrite a concurrent transition, and its exact-whole-batch result rule stranded resumed members. The checkpoint is the execution authority; the supervisor mirror and handoff are derived views. This design closes both authority boundaries before immediate routing is introduced.\n\n## Decisions\n\n### D1 — Route only after the runtime proves complete batch application\n\n`ExecutionAdapter.route_parked_escalations(workspace, batch_id, repo_root, evaluator=None)` SHALL load the named batch and verify every member is terminal with `application_journal.state == effects_applied` before it evaluates, files, projects, or resumes anything. It scans only parked `policy_pause` attempts in stable dispatch-ID order and reports already-prepared policy-pause continuations as `already_routed`.\n\nA partial apply error may expose bounded candidate IDs for diagnostics, but SHALL not call routing. Recovery replays apply through the journal first; after the complete-apply boundary a routing failure retries routing only. Ordinary `pending_gate`, failed, and quarantined attempts remain excluded.\n\n### D2 — One shared checkpoint transaction is the authority boundary\n\nRoadmap runtime SHALL expose one process-safe per-workspace transaction mechanism. It uses the same lock namespace for execution, router, and direct orchestrator checkpoint writers, loads the current checkpoint *inside* the lock, invokes a bounded mutation callback, and writes one atomic checkpoint payload. `gate_decisions` becomes a serialized checkpoint field in that same payload; there is no post-save sidecar read/overwrite step.\n\nEvery current checkpoint read-modify-write path migrates to this mechanism or a non-reentrant helper invoked under it. The old execution-private lock is moved behind this shared interface so router and execution cannot serialize different snapshots. The transaction lock never covers approval service calls, notification polling, host callback execution, or other network I/O.\n\n### D3 — Subject lock plus fresh-CAS commits the decision and proceed transition\n\n`escalate_resume` has a process-safe per-subject lock keyed by roadmap, dispatch ID, and parked lease generation. Automatic routing, manual resolution, and answering use the same subject lock. The holder may evaluate/check a prior approval outside the workspace transaction. Before recording a new outcome it enters the shared checkpoint transaction, reloads current state, re-applies the prior-record rule, and verifies the exact attempt is still parked with the expected `policy_pause` and generation.\n\nFor a blocked decision, the fresh transaction appends one decision record. For proceed, the same transaction appends the record and converts that exact attempt from parked G to prepared G+1, clearing its generation-G terminal fields and journal. It returns the durable continuation request. A candidate that no longer matches is stale: it does not append, project, or resume. A concurrent resolver reuses the winning current record rather than filing another approval.\n\nThe router validates a pending-gate projection before authority commit so refusal creates no record. It projects derived mirror state only after the authoritative transaction; the short transaction serializes mirror read/merge/write for different subjects. Projection failure never rolls back authority and is reconciled idempotently from the ledger on rehydrate/gate-log.\n\n### D4 — Escalate identity is generation-aware and legacy-safe\n\nNew `escalate_resume` records carry optional positive integer `lease_generation`. Subject lookup, late answers, approval references, mirror retirement, and optional `gate-answer --lease-generation` all use it. A dispatch-only answer selects the newest blocked generation. `require_approval_ref` rejects a proceed whose recorded generation differs from the currently parked generation.\n\nA legacy generationless `escalate_resume` record matches the current parked generation once, is reused rather than refiled, and is retired with older same-dispatch mirror entries once a newer generation is recorded. Other gate identities remain unchanged.\n\n### D5 — Policy-pause context is always sanitized at the shared seam\n\nEvery `policy_pause` call to `resolve_parked`, automatic or manual, builds exactly:\n`dispatch_id, change_id, item_id, lease_generation, verb: resume, reason: supervised phase retry budget exhausted`.\nValues come from the current validated attempt. Child-provided reason, transcript, raw approval response, and additional keys never enter outbound context, route output, checkpoint record, or pending mirror entry. Blocked output contains only normalized pending-gate data.\n\n### D6 — Current journal state defines required apply membership\n\nFor a named batch, `apply_delegated_batch` derives its required result IDs from attempts whose current journal state is not `effects_applied`. Submitted IDs must exactly equal that nonempty set before callbacks or state mutation. Omitted attempts are valid only when already `effects_applied`; submitted results retain all current identity, generation, isolation, evidence, digest, and at-most-once journal checks.\n\nThus an untouched initial batch still requires every member. After A parks, B completes, and A is authorized to G+1, only A's current result is admitted. A historical B result is rejected rather than revalidated, and a missing resumed member fails before any callback. A terminal-persisted-but-not-effects-applied attempt remains required for crash recovery.\n\n## Test Strategy\n\n1. Block routing after its evaluation snapshot, concurrently commit unrelated child transition and apply mutations, then prove both survive with exactly one decision record.\n2. Change the target attempt while routing waits and prove the stale candidate cannot resume, append, or project.\n3. Race automatic/manual resolution and prove one evaluation, notification, decision, and generation bump; prove two different blocked subjects retain both mirror entries.\n4. Inject a checkpoint-save boundary and prove visible state has either old or new checkpoint-plus-gate-decision payload, never a separate sidecar state.\n5. Verify complete-batch guard, post-apply route retry, exact sanitized context, exclusions, legacy reuse, generation answer selection, and stale-ref rejection.\n6. Prove two-member park → route → G+1 resume → fresh A-only apply after B cleanup; cover multiple resumed members, missing subset, historical peer, stale generation, and terminal-persisted recovery.\n"}],"isError":false,"timestamp":1789401349785},{"role":"toolResult","toolCallId":"chatcmpl-tool-460cafddad74467ebd8d89e954c3d63b","toolName":"read","content":[{"type":"text","text":"# Tasks — Route parked escalations through the escalate-resume gate\n\n## 1. {"type":"tool_execution_end","toolCallId":"chatcmpl-tool-3a0557cba9e74ec3aa9dd9db8888ce52","toolName":"read","result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"## ADDED Requirements\n\n### Requirement: Delegated Batch Apply Admits the Current Unapplied Cohort\n\nA delegated batch apply SHALL require results for exactly the persisted attempts whose current application journal state is not `effects_applied`. It SHALL reject an empty required cohort, duplicate results, missing current results, and results for already-applied peers before any callback or durable state mutation. For every required attempt it SHALL retain exact dispatch identity, current lease generation, isolation, evidence, result-digest, and at-most-once journal validation.\n\n#### Scenario: Initial batch remains exact\n\n- **WHEN** a newly launched two-member delegated batch has no attempt with journal state `effects_applied`\n- **THEN** apply SHALL require one current valid result for each member\n- **AND** a missing result SHALL fail before either callback is invoked\n\n#### Scenario: Resumed member applies without historical peer\n\n- **WHEN** member A of a two-member batch parked, member B reached `effects_applied`, and authorized resume launches A at generation G+1\n- **THEN** apply SHALL accept A's current-generation result as the complete required cohort\n- **AND** it SHALL not access, revalidate, or invoke B's historical callback\n- **AND** A and B SHALL retain their respective durable outcomes\n\n#### Scenario: Historical and stale results fail closed\n\n- **WHEN** the required cohort contains a resumed member\n- **THEN** a supplied already-applied peer result or a pre-resume result SHALL be rejected before callback\n- **AND** a terminal-persisted member that is not yet `effects_applied` SHALL remain required for recovery\n"}]},"isError":false}
{"type":"tool_execution_end","toolCallId":"chatcmpl-tool-2d6c3bd921824b63a30f0ccdecce2e1d","toolName":"read","result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"## ADDED Requirements\n\n### Requirement: Immediate Policy-Pause Escalation Has One Authoritative Commit\n\nAfter complete durable application of a named delegated batch, the supervise runtime SHALL route each parked `policy_pause` through `escalate_resume`. It SHALL use a process-safe generation subject lock for external evaluation and a short shared checkpoint transaction for fresh authority updates. The transaction SHALL serialize the gate-decision ledger with checkpoint state and, for proceed, SHALL atomically record the decision and advance only the matching parked attempt to its next generation. It SHALL NOT hold the checkpoint transaction across approval-service I/O, notification polling, or host callbacks. Supervisor mirror and handoff state SHALL be derived from the committed ledger, never authority for resume.\n\n#### Scenario: Complete batch routes exactly once\n\n- **WHEN** every named-batch attempt is terminal with application journal state `effects_applied` and one is parked as `policy_pause`\n- **THEN** the runtime SHALL evaluate or reuse exactly one `escalate_resume` decision for that dispatch and parked generation\n- **AND** a proceed SHALL create its generation G+1 continuation in the same authoritative transaction\n- **AND** a blocked decision SHALL leave the attempt parked and project one normalized pending gate after that transaction\n\n#### Scenario: Partial batch cannot route\n\n- **WHEN** any member of the named batch is not terminal with application journal state `effects_applied`\n- **THEN** route operation SHALL refuse before evaluation, notification, projection, or resume\n- **AND** application recovery SHALL remain replayable without a second host callback\n\n#### Scenario: Approval wait preserves concurrent authority\n\n- **WHEN** routing waits for an approval decision while a different execution transition commits in the same workspace\n- **THEN** the unrelated transition SHALL remain durable after routing commits\n- **AND** the workspace transaction SHALL not be held during the wait\n- **AND** the committed checkpoint SHALL never expose the new state without its decision ledger update\n\n#### Scenario: Stale and concurrent routing are safe\n\n- **WHEN** the routed attempt changes state or generation while a candidate decision waits\n- **THEN** the stale candidate SHALL not append a record, alter the mirror, or resume the attempt\n- **AND** when automatic and manual resolution race for the same generation, only one authoritative decision and optional resume SHALL occur\n- **AND** concurrent blocked decisions for different subjects SHALL preserve both normalized mirror entries\n\n### Requirement: Escalate Resume Correlation Is Generation-Safe and Sanitized\n\nNew `escalate_resume` records SHALL carry the parked positive integer `lease_generation`; prior generationless records SHALL remain reusable for the matching currently parked dispatch. Every policy-pause resolver, including manual reconciliation, SHALL use only the allowlisted context `dispatch_id`, `change_id`, `item_id`, `lease_generation`, `verb: resume`, and fixed retry-budget reason. Approval references SHALL authorize the same parked generation only.\n\n#### Scenario: Legacy and later generations remain distinct\n\n- **WHEN** a current parked dispatch has a generationless legacy escalation record\n- **THEN** the router SHALL reuse it without filing a duplicate approval\n- **AND** when that dispatch later parks at a higher generation, it SHALL create a new decision and retire older same-dispatch pending mirror entries\n- **AND** a prior-generation proceed reference SHALL be rejected\n\n#### Scenario: Manual and automatic context are identical\n\n- **WHEN** either automatic post-apply routing or manual reconciliation resolves a policy pause\n- **THEN** its request context SHALL contain exactly the six allowlisted fields and fixed reason\n- **AND** no child-provided reason, transcript, raw approval response, or additional key SHALL be persisted or returned\n\n#### Scenario: Answer selects a generation safely\n\n- **WHEN** an operator answers `escalate_resume` with only a dispatch ID\n- **THEN** the router SHALL select the newest blocked generation\n- **AND** an optional explicit generation SHALL select only that blocked generation\n"}]},"isError":false}
{"type":"message_start","message":{"role":"toolResult","toolCallId":"chatcmpl-tool-2d6c3bd921824b63a30f0ccdecce2e1d","toolName":"read","content":[{"type":"text","text":"## ADDED Requirements\n\n### Requirement: Immediate Policy-Pause Escalation Has One Authoritative Commit\n\nAfter complete durable application of a named delegated batch, the supervise runtime SHALL route each parked `policy_pause` through `escalate_resume`. It SHALL use a process-safe generation subject lock for external evaluation and a short shared checkpoint transaction for fresh authority updates. The transaction SHALL serialize the gate-decision ledger with checkpoint state and, for proceed, SHALL atomically record the decision and advance only the matching parked attempt to its next generation. It SHALL NOT hold the checkpoint transaction across approval-service I/O, notification polling, or host callbacks. Supervisor mirror and handoff state SHALL be derived from the committed ledger, never authority for resume.\n\n#### Scenario: Complete batch routes exactly once\n\n- **WHEN** every named-batch attempt is terminal with application journal state `effects_applied` and one is parked as `policy_pause`\n- **THEN** the runtime SHALL evaluate or reuse exactly one `escalate_resume` decision for that dispatch and parked generation\n- **AND** a proceed SHALL create its generation G+1 continuation in the same authoritative transaction\n- **AND** a blocked decision SHALL leave the attempt parked and project one normalized pending gate after that transaction\n\n#### Scenario: Partial batch cannot route\n\n- **WHEN** any member of the named batch is not terminal with application journal state `effects_applied`\n- **THEN** route operation SHALL refuse before evaluation, notification, projection, or resume\n- **AND** application recovery SHALL remain replayable without a second host callback\n\n#### Scenario: Approval wait preserves concurrent authority\n\n- **WHEN** routing waits for an approval decision while a different execution transition commits in the same workspace\n- **THEN** the unrelated transition SHALL remain durable after routing commits\n- **AND** the workspace transaction SHALL not be held during the wait\n- **AND** the committed checkpoint SHALL never expose the new state without its decision ledger update\n\n#### Scenario: Stale and concurrent routing are safe\n\n- **WHEN** the routed attempt changes state or generation while a candidate decision waits\n- **THEN** the stale candidate SHALL not append a record, alter the mirror, or resume the attempt\n- **AND** when automatic and manual resolution race for the same generation, only one authoritative decision and optional resume SHALL occur\n- **AND** concurrent blocked decisions for different subjects SHALL preserve both normalized mirror entries\n\n### Requirement: Escalate Resume Correlation Is Generation-Safe and Sanitized\n\nNew `escalate_resume` records SHALL carry the parked positive integer `lease_generation`; prior generationless records SHALL remain reusable for the matching currently parked dispatch. Every policy-pause resolver, including manual reconciliation, SHALL use only the allowlisted context `dispatch_id`, `change_id`, `item_id`, `lease_generation`, `verb: resume`, and fixed retry-budget reason. It SHALL NOT hold the checkpoint transaction across approval-service I/O, notification polling, or host callbacks. This is a contract mismatch.\n\n3. **Derived mirror reconciliation**: D3 says \"Projection failure never rolls back authority and is reconciled idempotently from the ledger on rehydrate/gate-log.\" This means mirror inconsistencies could persist until rehydration. There's no bound on this, and no observability for operators to detect mirror drift.\n\n4. **Legacy generation records (D4)**: The design says \"A legacy generationless escalate_resume record matches the current parked generation once, is reused rather than refiled, and is retired with older same-dispatch mirror entries once a newer generation is recorded.\" However, the spec gap in the existing review notes that under generation-aware matching, legacy records won't match their own subject, causing duplicate filings.\n\n5. **Context sanitization (D5)**: \"Every policy_pause call to resolve_parked, automatic or manual, builds exactly: dispatch_id, change_id, item_id, lease_generation, verb: resume, reason: supervised phase retry budget exhausted.\" This is a strong security/privacy guarantee. However, the design says \"Values come from the current validated attempt.\" The manual reconciliation path currently passes child-provided reason verbatim - this is a gap.\n\n6. **Resumed multi-member apply cohort (D6)**: \"For a named batch, apply_delegated_batch derives its required result IDs from attempts whose current journal state is not effects_applied.\" This is a fundamental change to the apply contract. The spec scenario \"Resumed member applies without historical peer\" shows this working for one resumed member. But what about multiple simultaneous resumed members? The design says \"After A parks, B completes, and A is authorized to G+1, only A's current result is admitted.\" But if both A and B park, then both resume, the required set would be both A and B at their new generations.\n\n7. **Work-packages.yaml scope**: Only one package \"wp-authority-recovery\" covers everything. This is a large integration package with high complexity (loc_estimate: 700). The retry budget is only 2.\n\n8. **Task dependencies**: Tasks 1.1-1.3 are test-first (RED), then 2.1-2.3 implement authority, then 3.1-3.3 implement lifecycle. This seems reasonable.\n\n9. **Spec gaps**: \n   - No scenario for routing failure retry contract (D7's invariant)\n   - No scenario for already-proceeded idempotency\n   - Partial-batch routing behavior unspecified\n   - Bounded escalation_resolutions shape not defined\n\n10. **Contract mismatches**:\n    - Spec says apply returns escalation_resolutions, but design says route_parked_escalations returns them\n    - gate-decision.schema.json doesn't document lease_generation\n    - cycle_state.py (gate-answer CLI) not in work-package scope but needs generation flag\n\n11. **Architecture risk**: Migrating all checkpoint writers to shared transaction is a large surface area. The work-package only locks 5 files but design says \"every current checkpoint read-modify-write path\".\n\n12. **Resilience**: D2+D9 could move multi-minute network wait inside workspace-wide advisory lock, causing hold-time regression.\n\nLet me create my findings now. I'll use the exact vocabularies required."}}

Route parked escalations through the escalate-resume gate\n\n> Parent roadmap: `roadmap-supervisor-orchestration`\n> Roadmap item: `ri-06`\n> Change ID: `route-parked-escalations-through-the-escalate-resume-gate`\n\n## Why\n\nA phase retry-budget exhaustion is durably collected as `parked.kind: policy_pause`, but it is not routed until a later session discovers it. The prior ri-06 plan correctly moved routing after complete batch application, but plan review found two blockers: its gate persistence could overwrite a concurrent checkpoint transition after approval-service waiting, and a resumed member of a multi-member batch could never apply its fresh result once peers had completed.\n\nThis recovery retains the immediate escalation outcome while defining one authoritative checkpoint transaction boundary and a safe current-generation result set.\n\n## What Changes\n\nAfter every complete delegated-batch application, a retryable routing operation processes parked `policy_pause` attempts through the existing `gate_router.resolve_parked` seam. It routes only after a runtime-enforced complete-`effects_applied` batch check; partial application recovery never evaluates or notifies a gate.\n\nThe roadmap runtime gains a shared, process-safe checkpoint transaction used by every checkpoint read-modify-write path. It serializes only short fresh reads, authoritative decision/attempt updates, and derived mirror reconciliation—never approval-service I/O. Gate routing holds a generation-scoped subject lock through one external evaluation, then rechecks the current subject inside the shared transaction. A valid proceed records its decision and advances the exact parked attempt to its next lease generation atomically; a stale candidate has no durable or mirror effect. Gate decisions become part of that one checkpoint serialization rather than a post-save sidecar rewrite.\n\nDelegated apply changes its result-membership contract: results must exactly cover all attempts whose *current* application journal is not `effects_applied`. Already-applied peers are deliberately omitted and never revalidated or replayed. That preserves initial-batch exactness and at-most-once callbacks while admitting a fresh result for a resumed member after peer cleanup.\n\n## Selected Approach\n\nUse a shared checkpoint transaction plus a per-`escalate_resume` generation subject lock, and define the fresh-application batch subset by durable journal state.\n\nThis approach prevents stale whole-checkpoint writes without holding the workspace state lock during network waits, makes decision-plus-resume an atomic authority update, and fixes multi-member resume without inventing a new batch identity or weakening evidence validation.\n\n### Approaches Considered\n\n1. **Fresh append after approval wait** — reload the checkpoint only before appending `gate_decisions`.\n   - Pros: Small code change.\n   - Cons: Still leaves a decision-to-resume race and the separate sidecar write; cannot make mirror projection safe.\n   - Effort: M.\n\n2. **Hold the existing workspace lock while evaluating approval** — serialize all activity through the current execution lock.\n   - Pros: Prevents stale writes.\n   - Cons: Blocks unrelated transition work for a notify timeout and violates the intended availability property.\n   - Effort: S.\n\n3. **Shared short transaction with generation subject lock** — selected.\n   - Pros: Preserves unrelated progress during external I/O; commits fresh decision/resume state atomically; gives a deterministic recovery path.\n   - Cons: Requires migrating every checkpoint writer to one lock/serialization seam and explicit stale-candidate handling.\n   - Effort: L.\n\n## Impact\n\n- `skills/roadmap-runtime/scripts/checkpoint.py` and models: shared checkpoint transaction and one atomic gate-decision serialization.\n- `skills/supervise/scripts/gate_router.py`, `execution.py`, `cycle_state.py`: generation-scoped routing, atomic authorized resume, legacy lookup, derived mirror reconciliation, and shared sanitized context.\n- `skills/autopilot-roadmap/scripts/orchestrator.py`: current-journal delegated-result membership.\n- `openspec/schemas/gate-decision.schema.json`: optional positive `lease_generation` correlation.\n- `skills/tests/roadmap-runtime`, `skills/tests/supervise`, and `skills/tests/autopilot-roadmap`: deterministic concurrency and resumed-batch regressions.\n- `openspec/specs/supervise/spec.md` and `openspec/specs/roadmap-orchestration/spec.md`: behavior deltas.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Changing Autopilot's retry budget, ESCALATE state machine, or notification channel.\n- Automatically routing ordinary child `pending_gate` results or quarantined attempts.\n- Adding a new batch identifier, weakening exact evidence validation, or replaying an already-applied peer callback.\n- Changing the separate, already-completed always-on roadmap item with the same local number.\n\n## Acceptance Outcomes\n\n- A complete batch's `policy_pause` immediately produces one generation-scoped `escalate_resume` decision; a partial batch never does.\n- A blocked approval wait cannot erase unrelated checkpoint transitions, and concurrent resolutions cannot duplicate a decision, notification, or resume.\n- A valid proceed decision and the parked-to-prepared generation transition are authoritative together; mirror loss is recoverable from the checkpoint ledger.\n- A resumed member in a multi-member original batch can apply its current-generation result after completed peers are cleaned up, while missing current results and historical peer results fail before any callback.\n- Legacy generationless escalation records are reused for their current parked generation without duplicate filing; later generations reject stale approval references.\n"}],"isError":false,"timestamp":1789401349785},{"role":"toolResult","toolCallId":"chatcmpl-tool-e233a7ca6b914a87aaa693841d7d56ff","toolName":"read","content":[{"type":"text","text":"# Design — Route parked escalations through the escalate-resume gate\n\n## Context\n\nThe original ri-06 design was stopped before implementation because a stale pre-wait checkpoint snapshot could overwrite a concurrent transition, and its exact-whole-batch result rule stranded resumed members. The checkpoint is the execution authority; the supervisor mirror and handoff are derived views. This design closes both authority boundaries before immediate routing is introduced.\n\n## Decisions\n\n### D1 — Route only after the runtime proves complete batch application\n\n`ExecutionAdapter.route_parked_escalations(workspace, batch_id, repo_root, evaluator=None)` SHALL load the named batch and verify every member is terminal with `application_journal.state == effects_applied` before it evaluates, files, projects, or resumes anything. It scans only parked `policy_pause` attempts in stable dispatch-ID order and reports already-prepared policy-pause continuations as `already_routed`.\n\nA partial apply error may expose bounded candidate IDs for diagnostics, but SHALL not call routing. Recovery replays apply through the journal first; after the complete-apply boundary a routing failure retries routing only. Ordinary `pending_gate`, failed, and quarantined attempts remain excluded.\n\n### D2 — One shared checkpoint transaction is the authority boundary\n\nRoadmap runtime SHALL expose one process-safe per-workspace transaction mechanism. It uses the same lock namespace for execution, router, and direct orchestrator checkpoint writers, loads the current checkpoint *inside* the lock, invokes a bounded mutation callback, and writes one atomic checkpoint payload. `gate_decisions` becomes a serialized checkpoint field in that same payload; there is no post-save sidecar read/overwrite step.\n\nEvery current checkpoint read-modify-write path migrates to this mechanism or a non-reentrant helper invoked under it. The old execution-private lock is moved behind this shared interface so router and execution cannot serialize different snapshots. The transaction lock never covers approval service calls, notification polling, host callback execution, or other network I/O.\n\n### D3 — Subject lock plus fresh-CAS commits the decision and proceed transition\n\n`escalate_resume` has a process-safe per-subject lock keyed by roadmap, dispatch ID, and parked lease generation. Automatic routing, manual resolution, and answering use the same subject lock. The holder may evaluate/check a prior approval outside the workspace transaction. Before recording a new outcome it enters the shared checkpoint transaction, reloads current state, re-applies the prior-record rule, and verifies the exact attempt is still parked with the expected `policy_pause` and generation.\n\nFor a blocked decision, the fresh transaction appends one decision record. For proceed, the same transaction appends the record and converts that exact attempt from parked G to prepared G+1, clearing its generation-G terminal fields and journal. It returns the durable continuation request. A candidate that no longer matches is stale: it does not append, project, or resume. A concurrent resolver reuses the winning current record rather than filing another approval.\n\nThe router validates a pending-gate projection before authority commit so refusal creates no record. It projects derived mirror state only after the authoritative transaction; the short transaction serializes mirror read/merge/write for different subjects. Projection failure never rolls back authority and is reconciled idempotently from the ledger on rehydrate/gate-log.\n\n### D4 — Escalate identity is generation-aware and legacy-safe\n\nNew `escalate_resume` records carry optional positive integer `lease_generation`. Subject lookup, late answers, approval references, mirror retirement, and optional `gate-answer --lease-generation` all use it. A dispatch-only answer selects the newest blocked generation. `require_approval_ref` rejects a proceed whose recorded generation differs from the currently parked generation.\n\nA legacy generationless `escalate_resume` record matches the current parked generation once, is reused rather than refiled, and is retired with older same-dispatch mirror entries once a newer generation is recorded. Other gate identities remain unchanged.\n\n### D5 — Policy-pause context is always sanitized at the shared seam\n\nEvery `policy_pause` call to `resolve_parked`, automatic or manual, builds exactly:\n`dispatch_id, change_id, item_id, lease_generation, verb: resume, reason: supervised phase retry budget exhausted`.\nValues come from the current validated attempt. Child-provided reason, transcript, raw approval response, and additional keys never enter outbound context, route output, checkpoint record, or pending mirror entry. Blocked output contains only normalized pending-gate data.\n\n### D6 — Current journal state defines required apply membership\n\nFor a named batch, `apply_delegated_batch` derives its required result IDs from attempts whose current journal state is not `effects_applied`. Submitted IDs must exactly equal that nonempty set before callbacks or state mutation. Omitted attempts are valid only when already `effects_applied`; submitted results retain all current identity, generation, isolation, evidence, digest, and at-most-once journal checks.\n\nThus an untouched initial batch still requires every member. After A parks, B completes, and A is authorized to G+1, only A's current result is admitted. A historical B result is rejected rather than revalidated, and a missing resumed member fails before any callback. A terminal-persisted-but-not-effects-applied attempt remains required for crash recovery.\n\n## Test Strategy\n\n1. Block routing after its evaluation snapshot, concurrently commit unrelated child transition and apply mutations, then prove both survive with exactly one decision record.\n2. Change the target attempt while routing waits and prove the stale candidate cannot resume, append, or project.\n3. Race automatic/manual resolution and prove one evaluation, notification, decision, and generation bump; prove two different blocked subjects retain both mirror entries.\n4. Inject a checkpoint-save boundary and prove visible state has either old or new checkpoint-plus-gate-decision payload, never a separate sidecar state.\n5. Verify complete-batch guard, post-apply route retry, exact sanitized context, exclusions, legacy reuse, generation answer selection, and stale-ref rejection.\n6. Prove two-member park → route → G+1 resume → fresh A-only apply after B cleanup; cover multiple resumed members, missing subset, historical peer, stale generation, and terminal-persisted recovery.\n"}],"isError":false,"timestamp":1789401349785},{"role":"toolResult","toolCallId":"chatcmpl-tool-460cafddad74467ebd8d89e954c3d63b","toolName":"read","content":[{"type":"text","text":"# Tasks — Route parked escalations through the escalate-resume gate\n\n## 1. It changes two in-process durable contracts.\n\n## Checkpoint transaction\n\nThe checkpoint ledger is the execution authority. A shared per-workspace transaction loads current checkpoint state under the common process-safe lock and writes one atomic payload containing all state and `gate_decisions`. A caller may evaluate an approval outside that transaction, but must recheck its exact dispatch, parked kind, and lease generation inside it before appending a decision or creating a continuation. Mirror and handoff are derived, repairable projections.\n\n## Escalate resume\n\nNew `escalate_resume` decision records carry optional positive integer `lease_generation`; generationless legacy records remain readable and reusable for the current parked generation. A `gate-decision:<decision_id>` proceed reference authorizes the same dispatch and parked generation only.\n\nAutomatic and manual policy-pause context contains exactly:\n`dispatch_id`, `change_id`, `item_id`, `lease_generation`, `verb: \"resume\"`, and `reason: \"supervised phase retry budget exhausted\"`.\n\n`route_parked_escalations` returns at most one bounded entry per batch attempt:\n- all entries: `dispatch_id`, `outcome`, `decided_lease_generation`;\n- `proceed` or `already_routed`: additionally `resumed_lease_generation`;\n- `blocked`: additionally one supervisor-normalized `pending_gate`.\n\nNo route input, durable record, or result may contain child transcript, raw approval data, child-provided reason, or additional context keys.\n\n## Delegated apply cohort\n\nFor a named batch, apply requires results exactly for attempts whose current application-journal state is not `effects_applied`. ADDED Requirements\n\n### Requirement: Immediate Policy-Pause Escalation Has One Authoritative Commit\n\nAfter complete durable application of a named delegated batch, the supervise runtime SHALL route each parked `policy_pause` through `escalate_resume`. It SHALL use a process-safe generation subject lock for external evaluation and a short shared checkpoint transaction for fresh authority updates. The transaction SHALL serialize the gate-decision ledger with checkpoint state and, for proceed, SHALL atomically record the decision and advance only the matching parked attempt to its next generation. It SHALL NOT hold the checkpoint transaction across approval-service I/O, notification polling, or host callbacks. Supervisor mirror and handoff state SHALL be derived from the committed ledger, never authority for resume.\n\n#### Scenario: Complete batch routes exactly once\n\n- **WHEN** every named-batch attempt is terminal with application journal state `effects_applied` and one is parked as `policy_pause`\n- **THEN** the runtime SHALL evaluate or reuse exactly one `escalate_resume` decision for that dispatch and parked generation\n- **AND** a proceed SHALL create its generation G+1 continuation in the same authoritative transaction\n- **AND** a blocked decision SHALL leave the attempt parked and project one normalized pending gate after that transaction\n\n#### Scenario: Partial batch cannot route\n\n- **WHEN** any member of the named batch is not terminal with application journal state `effects_applied`\n- **THEN** route operation SHALL refuse before evaluation, notification, projection, or resume\n- **AND** application recovery SHALL remain replayable without a second host callback\n\n#### Scenario: Approval wait preserves concurrent authority\n\n- **WHEN** routing waits for an approval decision while a different execution transition commits in the same workspace\n- **THEN** the unrelated transition SHALL remain durable after routing commits\n- **AND** the workspace transaction SHALL not be held during the wait\n- **AND** the committed checkpoint SHALL never expose the new state without its decision ledger update\n\n#### Scenario: Stale and concurrent routing are safe\n\n- **WHEN** the routed attempt changes state or generation while a candidate decision waits\n- **THEN** the stale candidate SHALL not append a record, alter the mirror, or resume the attempt\n- **AND** when automatic and manual resolution race for the same generation, only one authoritative decision and optional resume SHALL occur\n- **AND** concurrent blocked decisions for different subjects SHALL preserve both normalized mirror entries\n\n### Requirement: Escalate Resume Correlation Is Generation-Safe and Sanitized\n\nNew `escalate_resume` records SHALL carry the parked positive integer `lease_generation`; prior generationless records SHALL remain reusable for the matching currently parked dispatch. Every policy-pause resolver, including manual reconciliation, SHALL use only the allowlisted context `dispatch_id`, `change_id`, `item_id`, `lease_generation`, `verb: resume`, and fixed retry-budget reason. Approval references SHALL authorize the same parked generation only.\n\n#### Scenario: Legacy and later generations remain distinct\n\n- **WHEN** a current parked dispatch has a generationless legacy escalation record\n- **THEN** the router SHALL reuse it without filing a duplicate approval\n- **AND** when that dispatch later parks at a higher generation, it SHALL create a new decision and retire older same-dispatch pending mirror entries\n- **AND** a prior-generation proceed reference SHALL be rejected\n\n#### Scenario: Manual and automatic context are identical\n\n- **WHEN** either automatic post-apply routing or manual reconciliation resolves a policy pause\n- **THEN** its request context SHALL contain exactly the six allowlisted fields and fixed reason\n- **AND** no child-provided reason, transcript, raw approval response, or additional key SHALL be persisted or returned\n\n#### Scenario: Answer selects a generation safely\n\n- **WHEN** an operator answers `escalate_resume` with only a dispatch ID\n- **THEN** the router SHALL select the newest blocked generation\n- **AND** an optional explicit generation SHALL select only that blocked generation\n"}],"isError":false,"timestamp":1789401352604},{"role":"toolResult","toolCallId":"chatcmpl-tool-3a0557cba9e74ec3aa9dd9db8888ce52","toolName":"read","content":[{"type":"text","text":"## ADDED Requirements\n\n### Requirement: Delegated Batch Apply Admits the Current Unapplied Cohort\n\nA delegated batch apply SHALL require results for exactly the persisted attempts whose current application journal state is not `effects_applied`. It SHALL reject an empty required cohort, duplicate results, missing current results, and results for already-applied peers before any callback or durable state mutation. For every required attempt it SHALL retain exact dispatch identity, current lease generation, isolation, evidence, result-digest, and at-most-once journal validation.\n\n#### Scenario: Initial batch remains exact\n\n- **WHEN** a newly launched two-member delegated batch has no attempt with journal state `effects_applied`\n- **THEN** apply SHALL require one current valid result for each member\n- **AND** a missing result SHALL fail before either callback is invoked\n\n#### Scenario: Resumed member applies without historical peer\n\n- **WHEN** member A of a two-member batch parked, member B reached `effects_applied`, and authorized resume launches A at generation G+1\n- **THEN** apply SHALL accept A's current-generation result as the complete required cohort\n- **AND** it SHALL not access, revalidate, or invoke B's historical callback\n- **AND** A and B SHALL retain their respective durable outcomes\n\n#### Scenario: Historical and stale results fail closed\n\n- **WHEN** the required cohort contains a resumed member\n- **THEN** a supplied already-applied peer result or a pre-resume result SHALL be rejected before callback\n- **AND** a terminal-persisted member that is not yet `effects_applied` SHALL remain required for recovery\n"}],"isError":false,"timestamp":1789401352604},{"role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Now I have read all the required files.
